**The licence gate found six real things when it started looking at the extras trees.** Widening
the inventory from one production tree to eight install variants surfaced `multidict`,
`pycryptodome`, `regex`, `jsonalias`, and two `solders` versions — none of them problems, all
of them previously invisible. Four were free-text or historical spellings the allowlist did not
carry (`Apache License 2.0`, `BSD, Public Domain`, `CNRI-Python`); a comma in a licence field is
now read the conservative way, as though every listed licence applied. The two that declare no
licence at all are recorded in a small map that says **where** the licence was read from, not
just what it is, because "someone said MIT once" is not evidence. That six findings appeared the
moment the gate looked at what a user actually installs is the argument for O48 having been a
real finding rather than a pedantic one.

### S15c audit re-run — findings-only verdict and independent evidence

**Verdict: not release-ready.** S15c independently re-established that O44–O54 are fixed,
then found **four medium-severity findings** (O57–O60). There is no new critical or high
finding. Per §11.1 and §11.2, this session changes no product, test, workflow, documentation,
or configuration file; the next work is an ordinary remediation session, followed by another
findings-only audit re-run. S16 remains out of sequence.

The audit did not use S15b's regression files as its premise. It read the public contract first
and exercised the shipped surfaces directly:

- **Money dispositions:** direct calls to both language implementations' completion classifiers produce
  `paid-undelivered / committed / TX402_RESOURCE_DELIVERY` for a settled 403,
  `ambiguous / retained / TX402_PAYMENT_AMBIGUOUS` for a malformed settlement header on 200,
  and `ambiguous / retained / TX402_REDIRECT_BLOCKED` for a blocked paid redirect. A separate
  end-to-end TypeScript probe used a real local merchant and RPC stub: an injected store commit
  outage after a successful settlement raised non-retryable `ResourceDeliveryError` with
  `paid: true`, called no release, and left 50,000 atomic units reserved. O44, O46, O52, and O53
  therefore stay closed.
- **Policy scope:** the same paid-path probe observed `reserve.policyScope == "127.0.0.1"`, not
  a client-local identity. Both helpers collapse path and port for the same host and separate
  different hosts. That keeps O45 closed, but a Unicode IDN exposed the distinct O58:
  TypeScript normalizes `bücher.example` to `xn--bcher-kva.example`; Python's public helper
  returns `bücher.example`, while the Python client receives HTTPX's punycoded URL and stores
  `xn--bcher-kva.example`.
- **Clean artifacts:** `npm pack` produced the real 163,643-byte tarball. Four manual npm
  installs into empty temporary directories loaded the documented core, EVM, Solana, and signer
  surfaces; core-only correctly failed all three optional-chain imports. The packed CLI printed
  the documented help and `tx402 0.0.0`. A separately built Python wheel contains
  `spend_store_contract.py`, and `SpendStore`, `check_spend_store`, `assert_spend_store`, and
  `normalize_policy_host` import from `tx402`. O47, O51, and O54 stay closed.
- **Release gates:** the registry-backed supply-chain audit inventoried all eight install
  variants, licence-checked 130 shipped components, and found no blocking advisory; its eleven
  fail-closed negative cases pass. Reproducible builds were byte-identical. The performance
  gates passed at +0.012 ms non-402 overhead, 0.164 ms budget rejection, and +0.319 MiB over
  100,000 requests. O48 stays closed. Upstream remains `@x402/core`, `@x402/evm`, and
  `@x402/svm` 2.20.0 and Python `x402` 2.17.0, so O6 has no dependency-bump action.
- **Published surfaces:** `node tools/docs-live/index.js check --base https://docs.tx402.io`
  passes **8 / 8**, and `https://tx402.io/` returns final HTTP 200 HTML. O49 stays closed. The
  pushed baseline `0fad6c5` has **11 / 11 successful CI jobs** in run
  [#39](https://github.com/jaykomarraju/tx402-dev/actions/runs/30930021530). The separate docs
  run [#2](https://github.com/jaykomarraju/tx402-dev/actions/runs/30930020967) built everything
  and failed only at its explicit missing-credentials step, exactly O56; the live deployment is
  healthy, but CI cannot deploy until the user supplies those two repository secrets.
- **Size and accepted edge:** the current size measurement is unchanged at 17.20 / 25.00 KiB
  own code and 31.93 / 32.00 KiB total. O55 is assessed informational and nonblocking; remeasure
  before the next size-affecting change and amend ADR-008 only with recorded evidence. O43 is
  explicitly re-deferred: no new evidence turns the unreachable root-label host into a policy,
  origin, or money-safety bypass.

Verification was otherwise green: **498 TypeScript tests passed / 11 opt-in live tests skipped,
94.18 % statement and 90.61 % branch coverage; 522 Python tests passed at 92.47 % coverage,
with ruff and strict mypy clean; 67 conformance vectors; 4 / 4 packed install smokes; fuzz,
performance, size, supply-chain, and reproducibility gates pass.** On the repository-declared
Node 20.19.5, `pnpm check` reaches and passes every preceding check but its final Astro docs build
refuses that runtime; the same docs build emits all 17 pages under Node 24. That is O60, not a
failure of the published Node 20 SDK.

Still explicitly deferred, unchanged by this correlated agent audit: O8's independent human
security review, O10's registry trusted-publisher configuration, O12's release signing-key
rotation, and O56's Cloudflare repository secrets. None is authorized work for S15c.

### S15d — S15c remediation: what changed and how it was proven

**All four S15c findings are closed.** Every regression was written from the governing text —
SPEC §5.3, §6.7, §10, ADR-016 and ADR-018 — and then run against the S15c commit `38155c3` to
confirm it failed there before any product file was touched.

| Finding | Failing at `38155c3`                                             | Fix                                                                                                            |
| ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **O57** | TypeScript 4 of 18 new cases; Python 5 of the O57 group          | The settlement-header reader is pure. `payment.completed` is emitted from the disposition.                     |
| **O58** | Python 10 of the O58 group (TypeScript already canonical)        | ADR-018 amendment: the canonical host is the A-label, produced by one parser per language.                     |
| **O59** | The new claims gate reports **9 stale claims**; it reports 0 now | Eight documents corrected; `conformance:check` now scans them and `conformance:selftest` proves it can fail.   |
| **O60** | `pnpm check` cannot complete on the declared runtime             | Two declared floors, both derived: workspace 22.12+, published SDK 20.19+, checked by `tools/toolchain-check`. |

**O57 — a completion event is a statement about the disposition.** `readPaymentResponse` /
`_read_payment_response` now return evidence and emit nothing. The event moved to three
disposition-aware sites: the commit row emits it at `warn` with
`reason: payment-response-absent` when the merchant sent no metadata and at `info` otherwise,
the ambiguous row emits `paid: "unknown"` with `reason: payment-response-unparseable` when the
cause is a malformed envelope, and the released rows — a refusal, a re-challenge, an
unsuccessful settlement — emit nothing at all. The invariant the regressions assert is the one
that matters to an operator: **no event claims `paid: true` for a request whose error says
`paid: false`.** The money dispositions are untouched, which is what keeps this separate from
O44.

**O58 — one canonical host, derived rather than restated.** The finding was two helpers
documented as interchangeable returning different strings, so the fix could not be "make
Python punycode too" written twice. Python delegates to **httpx**, the parser that has already
converted the host of every request the SDK sends, which makes helper-versus-client drift
unrepresentable rather than merely corrected. Two smaller pre-existing divergences travelled
with it and are now aligned: an IPv6 literal keeps its brackets, and a trailing root dot is
stripped once rather than repeatedly. The eleven-row table is asserted verbatim in both
suites. **O43 is unchanged** — `https://./x` still normalizes to the empty string in both
languages, and the S15 judgment to accept it stands.

**O59 — the count is now derived, not repeated.** Eight release-facing documents said 65 while
the index said 67, and one test-merchant scenario still described the pre-ADR-016 behaviour of
a corrupt `PAYMENT-RESPONSE`. Correcting prose is worthless on its own — it was correct once
too — so `conformance:check` now scans the eight documents and the whole docs site for a
vector-count claim and fails when one disagrees with the index it just verified. `PLAN.md` and
`adr/` are deliberately **not** scanned: they are dated records, and "65 vectors" written about
S8 is true. Ten self-test cases pin what the scanner must and must not flag, including the
historical phrasing this file uses.

**O60 — two runtimes, both declared, neither restated.** The workspace floor is now
**Node 22.12+**, because `pnpm check` builds an Astro site; the published SDK floor stays
**Node 20.19+**, and CI still runs the whole TypeScript suite on Node 20 as well as 22.
`tools/toolchain-check` derives every rule from a file — the root manifest, `docs/package.json`,
**the installed Astro's own `engines`**, `packages/tx402/package.json`, and this repository's CI
matrix — so an Astro upgrade or a dropped matrix leg fails a check rather than making a document
wrong. It runs first in `pnpm check`, which turns a twelve-minute failure into a one-second one.

**Fixing it surfaced a sharper form of the same finding.** `.github/workflows/release.yml`'s
verify job ran `pnpm check` on Node 20. The **release gate itself** would have failed on the
tag, at its last step, after every other gate had passed — and nothing before a real tag would
have revealed it. That job now runs on the workspace runtime.

**S15d verification results:**

| Check                                                              | Result                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `pnpm check` (Node 22.23.1, CI order)                              | **clean end to end**, including the final 17-page docs build — the thing O60 said was impossible                                         |
| `pnpm toolchain:check`                                             | workspace >=22.12.0 (Astro), published SDK >=20.19.0 · fails with an actionable message on Node 20                                       |
| `pnpm conformance:check`                                           | **67 vectors** — M0:24 M1:12 M2:6 M3:7 M4:4 M5:6 M6:8, unchanged; **8 documents and the docs site agree on the count**                   |
| `pnpm conformance:selftest` / `pnpm toolchain:selftest`            | 10 and 11 negative cases behave as specified                                                                                             |
| TypeScript tests                                                   | **516 passed / 11 skipped** (opt-in live legs) — 498 at S15c plus this session's 18                                                      |
| TS coverage                                                        | **94.18 % statements / 90.66 % branch** — 90 % gate enforced                                                                             |
| Python lint / format / mypy                                        | clean; strict mypy over **46** files                                                                                                     |
| Python tests + coverage                                            | **544 passed**, **92.51 %** — 90 % gate enforced                                                                                         |
| `uv build`                                                         | sdist + wheel built                                                                                                                      |
| `pnpm size`                                                        | own **17.22 / 25 KiB**; total **31.95 / 32 KiB** — both PASS, and see O55: the tracking headroom is now **0.05 KiB**                     |
| `pnpm fuzz`                                                        | 25 000 iterations, 5 targets, 0 untyped failures                                                                                         |
| `pnpm perf` (Node 20)                                              | non-402 **+0.006 ms p95** (<15) · budget rejection **0.169 ms p95** (<2) · memory **−1.3 MiB over 100 000 requests** (<8) — **3/3 pass** |
| `pnpm perf` (Node 22)                                              | budget rejection **2.18 ms**, over the 2 ms gate — **and identically over it on unmodified `38155c3`**, which is O61                     |
| `pnpm supply-chain`                                                | 8 inventories licence-checked, no blocking advisory; 11 gate self-tests pass                                                             |

| GitHub Actions CI                                                  | **not yet run on this commit** — the S15e handoff must confirm it before drawing conclusions                                             |

**The perf gate is runtime-sensitive, and that is a finding rather than a failure.** On this
machine the budget-rejection gate reports 0.169 ms on Node 20 and 2.18 ms on Node 22 against a
2 ms threshold — and the plain-`fetch` baseline it is measured against moves the same way
(0.08 ms to 1.97 ms p95), so what moved is the runtime's loopback HTTP stack, not tx402. The
unmodified S15c commit fails it on Node 22 exactly as the current tree does, which is what
makes this an environmental property rather than an S15d regression. It is filed as **O61**,
and the release workflow's measured gates were kept on the published SDK's runtime rather than
being quietly re-pointed at a runtime the thresholds were never calibrated on.

**One incidental correction.** `pnpm install` rewrote `pnpm-lock.yaml` to add
`tools/supply-chain: {}` — an importer entry missing since that tool was added at S14. The
corrected lockfile is committed with this session.

**Explicitly re-deferred, unchanged by this session.** **O6** — upstream is still `@x402/core`,
`@x402/evm` and `@x402/svm` 2.20.0 and Python `x402` 2.17.0; no bump was performed, so the watch
stands. **O43** — the unreachable root-label host stays accepted, and the O58 work deliberately
preserved its behaviour in both languages rather than quietly changing it. **O55** — informational,
and now 0.05 KiB of tracking headroom rather than 0.07; the blocking own-code gate is unmoved at
17.22 of 25.00 KiB, and re-baselining ADR-008 remains a decision for the session that first needs
it, with the measurement recorded. **O56** — still USER: `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` and
`CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` as repository secrets. **O8, O10 and O12 are USER items** and were not
touched.

**The documentation site source changed** (the policy guide's IDN paragraph, the running
guide's vector count), so the deployed site is one commit behind until CI can deploy it. That
is O56's Cloudflare secrets, not new work: the live probes still pass 8 / 8 because no page was
added or removed.

### S15e — findings-only audit re-run: clean implementation verdict

**Verdict: no new product finding; the implementation audit is clean enough to proceed to
S16.** This is not yet permission to publish. SPEC §12.4 still requires O8's independent human
security review, O10's registry trusted-publisher setup, and O12's release-key generation;
O56 also keeps the separate docs workflow red on `main`. This session changed no product,
test, workflow, documentation, configuration, or ADR file. Only this living record changed.

**CI was confirmed before any audit conclusion.** On exact SHA
`a0fdc52280a7acee5074c554d4fc1467b8cab134`, CI run
[#41](https://github.com/jaykomarraju/tx402-dev/actions/runs/30952048438) is `completed / success`:
**11 / 11 jobs succeeded**, including both Node legs, Python 3.10–3.13, macOS, Windows,
conformance, docs build, and release gates. Separate Docs run
[#3](https://github.com/jaykomarraju/tx402-dev/actions/runs/30952048356) is
`completed / failure`: its build, generated-page, site, and deployable-root steps succeeded;
the step named **`A deploy from main requires the deploy credentials`** failed, and deploy plus
the published-site probe were skipped. That is the exact fail-closed O56 condition, not a
product regression.

The re-audit began from SPEC/ADRs and exercised the public contract instead of treating the
S15/S15c regression files as proof:

- **O44/O52/O53:** both shipped completion classifiers agree on all eight independently
  selected branches. Successful settlement on 403 is `paid-undelivered / committed /
TX402_RESOURCE_DELIVERY`; a malformed present response on 200 is `ambiguous / retained /
TX402_PAYMENT_AMBIGUOUS`; a blocked redirect is `ambiguous / retained /
TX402_REDIRECT_BLOCKED`; absent evidence on 403 releases, absent evidence on 200 commits,
  and transport after signature retains. A separate high-level paid redirect sent exactly
  one signed request, raised public `PaidRedirectBlockedError`, and retained all 50,000 atomic
  units. The redirect target received no follow-up.
- **O45/O58/O43:** the real TypeScript paid path reserved under `127.0.0.1`; a second client
  sharing the same store hit the same cap before another signer call. Both public helpers
  produced the same A-label/IPv6/port/path/trailing-dot table, including
  `bücher.example` → `xn--bcher-kva.example`. The accepted root-label input still produces
  the empty host in both languages and remains non-routing and fail-closed for specific
  allowlists, so O43's S15 assessment stands.
- **O46/O57:** the real local TypeScript merchant + EVM RPC paid call delivered 200 with one
  reservation, one signature, one paid retry, and 50,000 committed atomic units. A refused
  paid request released to zero and emitted only `request.failed / paid:false`; an injected
  post-settlement commit outage raised non-retryable `ResourceDeliveryError / paid:true`,
  called no release, and retained 50,000. An independent Python paid-path probe produced the
  same refusal and commit-outage contract. Direct completion branches emit completion only
  from commit/ambiguous dispositions, never from refusal or re-challenge.
- **O47/O51/O54:** the real packed npm tarball passed all four clean install modes. Core loaded
  `tx402` and its CLI but correctly refused EVM, Solana, and signer entry points; the three
  documented optional installs loaded exactly the entry points they advertise, and the CLI
  printed `tx402 0.0.0`. A separately built wheel installed with dependencies into an
  isolated Python 3.13 virtual environment, ran `tx402 --version`, publicly imported
  `MemorySpendStore` and `check_spend_store`, and passed the reusable store contract. The
  wheel contains `tx402/spend_store_contract.py`.
- **O48/O49/O50/O59/O60:** the registry-backed supply-chain gate inventoried all eight install
  variants, licence-checked 130 distinct shipped components, scanned 63 npm names plus all
  four Python variants, found no blocking advisory, and passed all eleven fail-closed
  fixtures. Two clean builds were byte-identical. All eight required docs pages and the
  `https://tx402.io/` homepage return final 200 HTML. The conformance gate counted 67 vectors
  and found all eight documents plus the docs site current; its ten negative fixtures passed.
  The Node 22.23.1 aggregate gate reports workspace >=22.12 and published SDK >=20.19 and
  completes through the 17-page docs build.

**O61 is resolved with Linux-runner measurements, without a rebaseline.** The exact SHA's
`ubuntu-latest` release-gates job ran the full Node 20 harness and recorded non-402 overhead
**0.033 ms p95**, budget rejection **0.574 ms p95** including the loopback 402, and memory
growth **−0.596 MiB** over 100,000 requests — 3 / 3 pass. More importantly, the exact SHA's
Ubuntu/Node 22 job ran T-006's direct 500-sample in-memory policy evaluation and passed its
**p95 <2 ms** assertion while proving no store or signer call occurred. O61's own decision
rule said an unaffected Node 22 CI leg makes the macOS 2.18 ms result a local runtime/loopback
artifact; that condition is met. The harness, threshold, and release workflow remain
unchanged.

### S15f — documentation visual alignment: presentation only

**Scope: how the documentation looks, and nothing else.** The docs and the landing page at
`tx402.io` are one product and were two visual systems. This session made them one. It is not
S16, it is not a content pass, and it changed no product behaviour, route, or workflow.

**What changed — three paths, and that is the whole list.**

| Path                        | Change                                                                              |
| --------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `docs/src/styles/tx402.css` | 33 lines → the full theme: tokens, header, sidebar, TOC, prose, code, tables        |
| `docs/public/fonts/*.woff2` | Inter + JetBrains Mono, the same latin variable subsets the landing serves (80 KiB) |
| `docs/public/favicon.svg`   | the tx402 route mark                                                                |

`docs/src/content/**`, `README.md`, both package READMEs, the generated error and API pages,
SPEC, PRD, the ADRs, CHANGELOG, VERSIONING, SECURITY, CONTRIBUTING and CODE_OF_CONDUCT are
byte-identical: `git diff -- docs/src/content README.md packages/tx402/README.md` is empty, and
the whole working tree at commit time was those three paths plus this file.

**The approach was to re-point tokens, not to write selectors.** Starlight's components are
written against `--sl-color-gray-1…7`, so the landing palette is mapped onto that scale once and
the rest follows. Custom CSS is emitted **outside** Starlight's `@layer starlight.*`, so it wins
at equal specificity and the file contains no `!important`. No rule targets an `astro-*` class:
those hashes change every build.

**Where the two designs disagree, the documentation won.** Code blocks keep Expressive Code's
per-scheme syntax theme rather than staying dark in light mode as the landing article does —
only the frame, border, radius and tab bar were re-skinned. There is no scroll-triggered motion,
no marketing hero density, and the only animation is hover/focus transitions, which
`prefers-reduced-motion` neutralises.

**Four defects were found and fixed during the session; three were mine.**

1. **Tables drew short of their own frame.** `display: block` makes a table its own scroll
   container, but the internal table box still shrink-wraps, so at `width: 100%` the header fill
   and row rules stopped short of the border and left a dead strip. Fixed with `width:
fit-content; max-width: 100%`.
2. **The mobile menu button rendered as an empty box.** Starlight ships it as a filled disc —
   page-coloured glyph on a text-coloured fill. Re-skinning the fill without setting the glyph
   colour left a `#0a0c11` icon on a `#12161f` surface.
3. **A border on the search button drew a rule across the mobile header**, because below 50rem
   Starlight's open-modal button is a bare icon stretched by the header's flex row. The border is
   now scoped to the breakpoint that gives the button a box.
4. **`/favicon.svg` 404'd**, and had since the site was first built: Starlight emits no default
   favicon but references one. Not filed as an open item — it is a missing presentation asset,
   fixed in the same session by the same change that gave the docs the brand mark.

5. **A second rule was drawn across the top bar**, reported by the user after the first deploy.
   Starlight nests a `<div class="header">` inside its `<header class="header">`, so the bare
   class selector `.header` styled both: the inner one is inset by the nav padding, so its
   border painted a shorter line through the middle of the bar and its background applied a
   second coat of the translucent fill. Now scoped to `header.header`. A DOM probe over every
   horizontal edge in the header confirms one full-width edge at 500 px and at 1440 px, where
   before there were two.

A sixth, cosmetic: pages that separate sections with `---` printed the authorial rule and the
automatic section rule as two lines. The automatic rule now yields to an explicit one.

**Verification was measured, not eyeballed.** A local static server rendered the built site;
phone viewports go through an exactly-sized same-origin iframe because headless Chrome will not
open a window narrower than 500 px.

| Check                                                             | Result                                                                                                                                                            |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Horizontal overflow, 17 pages × dark/light × 390 px               | **0 of 34** overflow; the probe was proved to have teeth against a deliberately 2000 px-wide fixture                                                              |
| Focus ring, 9 interactive controls × both themes                  | 2 px solid accent at 3 px offset on **every** one                                                                                                                 |
| Contrast of introduced label colours                              | landing `--text-faint` measured **4.29:1** dark / **4.21:1** light — under 4.5:1, so all seven small-label uses moved to `--text-muted` (**7.64:1** / **6.24:1**) |
| Body and link colours                                             | body **13.41:1** dark / **12.16:1** light; links **7.45:1** / **5.48:1**                                                                                          |
| Wide tables and long code blocks                                  | scroll inside their own container on every page at 390 px                                                                                                         |
| Sidebar, TOC, mobile menu, search dialog                          | screenshotted open in both themes; all still operable                                                                                                             |
| `git diff -- docs/src/content README.md packages/tx402/README.md` | empty                                                                                                                                                             |
| `pnpm docs:check`                                                 | generated pages current                                                                                                                                           |
| `pnpm check` (Node 22.23.1)                                       | green end to end, including the 17-page docs build                                                                                                                |
| TypeScript tests / coverage                                       | **516 passed**, 11 skipped; **94.18 % statements / 90.66 % branch**                                                                                               |
| Python ruff / format / mypy / pytest                              | clean; **544 passed**, **92.51 %**                                                                                                                                |
| `pnpm size`                                                       | own **17.22 / 25.00 KiB**; total **31.95 / 32.00 KiB** — PASS                                                                                                     |
| `tx402-landing` working tree                                      | byte-identical to its initial dirty state; never written to                                                                                                       |

`tx402-landing` was read only. Its seven modified and four untracked files were fingerprinted
by SHA-256 at session start and again at the end, and are unchanged; the fonts and favicon were
copied out of it, nothing was copied in.

**S16 must not install from the registries.** Neither package is published, so
`npm install tx402` or `uv pip install tx402` would fail or fetch something unrelated. A tarball
and a wheel were built from the final committed tree, verified by installing each into an empty
temporary environment, and left **outside the repository**; their absolute paths, SHA-256
digests, source commit, and build commands are carried in the S16 handoff prompt. They are not
committed and not published. Substituting those paths where the documentation says `tx402` is
declared setup, **not a UX finding**; every other documented instruction is followed literally.
The S16 stopwatch starts after that bootstrap, when the cold agent begins following the docs.

**CI on the pushed commit `71c13dd` is red, and one of the two red signals is new.**

- **Docs run [#4](https://github.com/jaykomarraju/tx402-dev/actions/runs/30975316819)** —
  `completed / failure`. Build, generated-page, site and deployable-root steps all succeeded;
  the step `A deploy from main requires the deploy credentials` failed and deploy was skipped.
  That is exactly O56's fail-closed condition and **not** a regression.
- **CI run [#43](https://github.com/jaykomarraju/tx402-dev/actions/runs/30975316817)** —
  `completed / failure`, **10 of 11 jobs green**. Both Node legs, all four Python versions,
  macOS, Windows, conformance parity and the docs-site job passed. `Release gates (fuzz, perf,
supply chain)` failed at its first measured step, `Fuzz and property gates`. That is
  **O62**, filed below: a seed-dependent contradiction between `normalizePolicyHost`'s
  documented one-dot rule and the fuzz gate's idempotence assertion. It reproduces
  identically on the parent commit `157ca77` in a clean worktree, so S15f did not cause it —
  the gate is time-seeded and prior sessions were lucky. It was **not fixed here**: changing
  policy-host normalization is product behaviour, which this session was not authorised to
  touch.

**One open item filed; the rest re-deferred.** **O62** is new and release-blocking. It is the
only O62+ item, and it was filed only because it was independently reproduced — twice, and on
a commit this session did not write. **O6** stays on its
watch — installed pins remain `@x402/*` 2.20.0 and Python `x402` 2.17.0 while the registries
advertise 2.21.0 and 2.18.0; a later implementation session must read the changelogs and replay
the conformance and artifact gates before any bump, and a styling session is not that session.
**O43** remains accepted and nonblocking. **O55** remains informational: total-size headroom is
still **0.05 KiB**, untouched here because documentation assets are not in the SDK bundle.
**O56** remains USER — the separate docs workflow will fail again on this push at `A deploy from
main requires the deploy credentials`, which is the fail-closed condition working, not a
regression. **O8**, **O10** and **O12** remain USER release blockers.

**The restyled site was published manually, on the user's explicit in-session authorization**,

**This does not close O56.** Nothing about the repository's ability to deploy changed: the

### S16 — fresh-eyes UX pass (§11.3): cold start, findings only — **did not come back clean**

**Verdict: the quickstart's five-minute claim fails at 88 seconds, on step 2, and never
recovers.** A stranger following the documentation literally cannot reach a settled payment on
either chain — not slowly, but at all. Session total was 12m29s, and that number is **not** a
time-to-value figure: payments settled only after the agent stepped outside the documentation
to repair the merchant's challenge by hand. §11.3 therefore has **not** been discharged, and
under §11.1 the stage must be **re-run** after remediation.

Setup was excluded from the clock and both artifact digests were verified. Packages were
installed per the docs' own rows — `tx402` (local tarball) + `@x402/evm` + `viem`, and the
Solana trio, for TypeScript; `tx402[all]` (local wheel) for Python.

**Nine findings, filed in §9 as O63–O71.** Two are blockers. **O63** — the public repository
the quickstart clones is empty — is a USER item and is the reason every finding below step 2
was reachable only by substituting the local checkout. **O64** — the documented test merchant
emits `extra: {}`, which tx402 itself rejects — is the more interesting of the two, and is a
process failure rather than three edits: the docs' own base-testnet troubleshooting table
already named this exact cause, a second docs page asserts the local merchant "cannot exercise
settlement" while the quickstart asserts it does, and `tools/ttv` — the harness that measures
SPEC §16 and reports PASS — never touches `DEFAULT_REQUIREMENTS` at all, because it builds its
own requirement object with a correct `extra`. The gate that exists to prove the quickstart
works structurally bypasses the object the quickstart tells users to run. The pages were
verified separately and never end to end.

**Positive evidence, on real testnets, 50000 atomic units each.** Once a valid challenge
reaches it, the SDK is excellent — both of these settled on the first try:

| Chain         | Transaction                                                                                |
| ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Base Sepolia  | `0x34ca581ffe33a30bd9486ed18e362a65921ead138e6afcc4f267cadd21c79f85`                       |
| Solana Devnet | `3xK9bSwuEwjHf92J7Et5CryyGzH4vxyZB4GYicDRpTJY3JH8y4SoeugYz8fgZBpaVMUgSSBdmG1JKu7bEE9sPf74` |

**Five properties S16 confirmed empirically, which any remediation must not regress.** These
are recorded as positive evidence because a fix for the findings above is exactly the kind of
change that could quietly break them, and S17 pins each with a test:

- **Cross-language parity is real.** The TypeScript and Python CLIs emitted **byte-identical**
  `--json`, down to the same `headerHash`.
- **`--json` matches the spec on success and on failure**, and `details.schemaPath`
  `/accepts/*/extra` pointed straight at the merchant defect — the machine-readable output
  diagnosed O64 faster than the prose did.
- **Ordering holds**: `policy.checked` → `budget.reserved` → `sign.started`, observed, not
  asserted.
- **Secrets stay out of logs.** The event stream carried `settlementIdHash`; the raw
  `settlementId` was reachable only through the ledger API.
- **Deterministic multi-chain ranking worked exactly as promised.**

**This session filed findings and fixed nothing**, per §11.2's posture rule and §11.1's
sequencing. Remediation is S17.

### S17 — S16 remediation: eight of nine findings closed, O63 escalated

**Findings were filed into §9 before anything was fixed**, so the record of what S16 found is
independent of what this session chose to do about it.

**Every regression was derived from the finding and run against `00c1685` first.** A test that
passes before the fix is not evidence.

| Suite                                                    | Before             | After         |
| :------------------------------------------------------- | :----------------- | :------------ |
| `packages/tx402/test/ux-regressions-s16.test.ts`         | 21 failed / 1 pass | **22 passed** |
| `packages/tx402-python/tests/test_ux_regressions_s16.py` | 10 failed / 1 pass | **11 passed** |

The one test passing in each suite beforehand is deliberate: "still accepts a complete logger"
is the guard against over-fixing, and it must hold in both states.

**Per finding.**

| #       | Decision and what changed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| :------ | :------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **O63** | **Not fixed — escalated, by the user's decision.** Every URL pointing at `neogeeks/tx402` was left **exactly as it is**: the public repository is not to be touched until after the §11.3 re-run, and the layout of that repository is to be designed only once the re-run is clean. Repointing now would be work done twice against a tree that does not exist. The §11.3 re-run must therefore be handed the local checkout as declared setup, exactly as S16 was, and **its handoff prompt must say so**. Link re-verification at publish time is recorded in §11.4.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **O64** | **Fixed in the fixture.** All four `DEFAULT_REQUIREMENTS` sets now carry the `extra` their chain family needs — EIP-712 domain for EVM (per-network name: `USD Coin` on mainnet, `USDC` on Sepolia), fee payer for SVM. `cli.js` additionally re-reads the fee payer from the facilitator's `/supported` when `--facilitator` is given, so a rotated fee payer cannot silently break the documented path; an unreachable facilitator keeps the built-in value rather than refusing to start. **The contradiction was resolved, not left standing:** `operations/base-testnet.mdx` said the local merchant "cannot exercise settlement" — that is now scoped to _that suite_, which runs without a facilitator, and states plainly that the same merchant settles for real when given one.                                             |
| **O65** | **Fixed in the docs; the CLI is right.** SPEC §4.3 specifies `policy.allowedNetworks` as defaulting to production networks, and SPEC §16 forbids silent fallback from production to testnet — a default that accepted Base Sepolia would violate both. The quickstart now passes `--network` in every command and explains why in a note; the CLI guide carries a caution; both CLIs' `--help` now says the flag is required for a testnet.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **O66** | **Fixed in the docs; the CLI is right.** SPEC §11 requires `--dry-run` to _plan routes_, and a route cannot be ranked without pricing it, so a configured key is necessary. The clause forbids only _invoking a signer_, which `cli/run.ts` enforces structurally. The key step is now **step 3**, before the dry run at step 4, and the false promise "No key, no signature, no money" is now the true and narrower "No signature and no money".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **O67** | **Fixed.** Exit 5's row no longer sends the reader nowhere. A second table maps each of the four exit-5 messages to what is actually wrong, and says which two are **merchant** defects the reader cannot fix.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **O68** | **Fixed without weakening anything.** All four examples opt into localhost, derived from the URL rather than hardcoded, so copying an example carries no relaxation. The guard itself is untouched and still permits `http://` only on `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`::1`, and only when asked.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| **O69** | **Fixed in the examples; the SDK floor is unchanged at `>=20.19.0`.** `examples/typescript/package.json` now declares its own `engines.node: ">=22.6.0"`, which is what `--experimental-strip-types` actually requires, and the new examples README states the distinction.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **O70** | **Fixed.** The dangling `agent-loop` script is gone. A regression asserts every script names a file that exists, so the next one fails a test rather than a user.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **O71** | **Fixed, all three parts.** `examples/README.md` now exists and documents `TX402_MERCHANT_URL` — previously the only place that name appeared was the error you got for not setting it. **(a)** A structurally invalid logger is now refused at construction in both languages, mirroring the `spendStore` check that was already there; `emit`'s suppression is correct and stays, because a logger fault must never fail a settled payment. The configuration reference no longer describes an object as though it were a callback. **(b)** `tools/docs-gen` now enumerates the client's methods from a **constructed instance** with signatures read from the shipped `.d.ts`, so `inspect`, `plan`, `getBudgetState` and `queryBudgetState` appear on the site for the first time. The generator was fixed, not the emitted file. |
| **O62** | **Resolved on the gate side, and the gate got stronger.** `normalizePolicyHost` is unchanged: the one-dot rule is what ADR-018 documents, `a.test..` is not a resolvable name, and changing it would move the spend-ledger scope key for no gain. The fuzz gate now checks the contract **from the input side** — an output-side check cannot see this rule at all, since a strip-everything normalizer emits hosts that never end in a dot. Verified by mutation: strip-all and strip-none are both now rejected and the shipped normalizer passes, none of which the old assertion could distinguish. **Six `policy.host-normalization` conformance vectors** pin it across both languages, including `double-dot` for the exact disputed case. ADR-018 carries an S17 amendment recording all of it.                               |

**Fuzz, measured rather than asserted: 0 failures in 290 runs** — 250 deterministic seeds plus
40 wall-clock-seeded runs at CI's `TX402_FUZZ_N=200000`, against a pre-fix rate of 10 in 200
(5.0%). The originally filed seed `1345513073` now passes.

**A real settled payment on both chains, through the repaired quickstart path, following the
documentation literally** — merchant command, `--network`, key, dry run, paid call. Both
verified **on-chain by RPC** rather than trusted from the status line:

| Chain         | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| :------------ | :-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Base Sepolia  | `0xc8daca299b5c408bb893534a706f83831515fb13b17f3155d3a290d93618b660` — 50000 atomic from `0xaad1566216D2447B530E04945dfEefD04C84967B` to the documented `payTo`                                                                                                 |
| Solana Devnet | `5EYK1pLUhf9KtY4fhzM6ZBtRqrwiKqBBgeqCubMHNE5RFX6pjSotu3JEDKuC3QQn8NfmA1tHJHHWeJ3KCKaewAV8` — `err: null`, slot 481327854, payer 29948000 → 29898000, recipient 476803020 → 476853020, fee paid by the facilitator's `CKPKJWNdJEqa…` so **buyer SOL delta is 0** |

**Gates.** Full `pnpm check` green on Node 22.23.1 — all twenty stages, including
`docs:check`, `docs:build` (17 pages), `conformance:check` at **73 vectors**, and `size`.
Python: ruff check, ruff format, mypy (47 files) and pytest **567 passed at 92.52%**.
TypeScript **550 passed / 11 skipped**. `supply-chain` PASS, `reproducible` PASS.

**Two things a later session must not be surprised by.**

- **The total-size number now has _zero_ headroom.** O71(a)'s validation initially took the
  core import path to 32.01 KiB gz against ADR-008's 32.00 KiB total and **failed the gate**.
  It was rewritten to a four-string loop rather than re-baselining the ceiling, and now
  measures **exactly 32.00**. It passes; the next byte on that path does not. The blocking
  own-code gate is unaffected at 17.27 / 25.00 KiB. See O55.
- **The vector count is a cross-document gate.** Adding six vectors required updating the
  count in eight documents plus the docs site; `conformance:check` fails otherwise.

**CI on the pushed commit `c7fbd75`: run [#47](https://github.com/jaykomarraju/tx402-dev/actions)
is green, 11 / 11 jobs** — both Node legs, all four Python versions, macOS, Windows,
conformance parity, the docs-site job, and **Release gates (fuzz, perf, supply chain)**, which
is the job O62 had been intermittently reddening. The separate **Docs run #6 fails as
designed**: build, generated-page, site and deployable-root steps all succeeded, and only
`A deploy from main requires the deploy credentials` failed, with deploy skipped. That is
O56's fail-closed condition working and **not** a product regression.

**The docs were published manually, on the user's explicit in-session authorization, and this
was a precondition for S18 rather than a convenience.** S17 changed the quickstart, the CLI
guide, the base-testnet page, the configuration reference and the generated API page, which
left `docs.tx402.io` serving the **pre-S17** text — verified directly: the live quickstart
still carried the false "No key, no signature" promise and no `--network` flag. §11.3 sends a
cold agent to read the documentation site, so starting S18 against that site would have made
it re-find O65, O66 and O67 — three findings that are already fixed — and the re-run would
have failed for reasons that are no longer true.

Rebuilt from the committed tree (`docs:check` current, 17 pages) and deployed with
`wrangler pages deploy docs/dist --project-name tx402-docs --branch main`, the same project
and command the workflow uses, from the user's own authenticated Cloudflare account. Verified
over the public internet afterwards: `docs:live` **8 / 8**, the live quickstart now carrying
`--network eip155:84532` and "No signature and no money", the stale promise gone (0
occurrences), and `inspect`, `getBudgetState` and `queryBudgetState` present on the API page
for the first time. The `.wrangler/` scratch directory was removed and the tree left clean.

**This does not close O56.** The repository still cannot deploy itself: the credentials live
only on one developer's machine, the workflow still has no `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, and the
next push to `main` will fail exactly the same way. A manual deploy is a published site, not
a pipeline.

**O6, O43, O55 and O56 are re-deferred**, and **O8, O10 and O12 remain USER release blockers.**
Nothing here touched registries, keys, or Cloudflare.

**§11.3 is not discharged.** S16 did not come back clean, so under §11.1 the fresh-eyes UX pass
must be **re-run cold, by a new agent**, before publication is considered. This session's own
end-to-end testing does **not** discharge it: the agent that repaired the quickstart cannot
un-know it, which is the entire reason §11.1 requires a re-run rather than a self-check.

### S18 — fresh-eyes UX pass re-run (§11.3): cold start, findings only — **did not come back clean**

2026-08-05. The S17 fixes were re-tested cold by an agent that had not read `PLAN.md`,
`SPEC.md`, `PRD.md`, `adr/` or any source file, against the manually published docs site.

**Five findings, filed here as O72–O76. Two are blockers.** None of them is a re-find of an
S16 item: O65, O66 and O67 stayed fixed, and the S16 blockers did not recur. The pass also
confirmed a large body of behaviour as correct — CLI steps 1–5 verbatim on both chains first
try, `--dry-run` never signing and never reserving, the dev-key warning on every read of both
variables, stdout carrying only the response body, the facilitator broadcasting so the buyer's
outbound transaction count stays 0, exit 3 and exit 5 reproducing as documented, settlement
evidence outranking the status line, the CLI guide's `*)` catch-all, all 23 documentation
links resolving 200, and TS↔Python parity holding including in failure modes.

**S18 was findings-only and wrote nothing to the repository**, which is why the five arrive
here unnumbered and are given O-numbers by S19 rather than by the session that found them.

### S19 — S18 remediation: all five findings closed

2026-08-05, on branch `fix/s19-ux-remediation`. **All five of O72–O76 are resolved**, in both
languages wherever the item is language-bearing.

**CI was measured, not inherited.** The commit log's "run #47 green 11/11" was a claim about
`7376245` carried forward through a session that never executed a gate. S19 ran the gates
itself before changing anything: at `7376245`, `pnpm check` completed green on Node v22.23.1
and the Python gates completed green — ruff, ruff format, mypy strict over 47 source files,
and 567 pytest tests at 92.52 % coverage against the 90 % floor. Both were re-run green after
the fixes. The two runtimes are the ones the toolchain contract names: the workspace gate on
Node 22 because it ends in an Astro build, and every reproduction on **Node v20.19.5**, the
published SDK's floor and the runtime S18 used — the top-level-await failure in O73 is
sensitive to that choice and does not reproduce identically on Node 22.

**Every finding was reproduced before it was fixed**, against the local tarball and wheel
built from the committed tree and installed into throwaway environments outside the
repository, exactly as the declared pre-publication substitution requires.

**O74 is the one that required a decision rather than a repair**, and it is recorded as
**ADR-019**. The settlement identifier already reached the client; the question was whether it
reaches the operator, given that SPEC §10 deliberately _hashes_ it in the event stream while
SPEC §5.3 deliberately keeps it _raw_ on the buyer's own ledger entry. `--json` was ruled to
be the ledger's trust boundary, not the aggregator's. `schemaVersion` deliberately stays at
`1`: the change is purely additive and bumping it would falsify the field's documented meaning
while breaking every script pinned to `1`.

**Verified against real money on both chains.** Base Sepolia settlements
`0xd401286cff321fc13768b0a3f775415710ecdc22c2bf2fd2f38f573d8c692b4c`,
`0x7a1fce5d4eafc00cf5af0c66afc70315de4254ac442ab37e214cb954f02e513d` (the exit-9 case) and
`0x8a01f2027e5af993977c5c4c7dded4e8a031aa0a07578aa2f5d429f670af5af0`, all 50000 atomic, all
`status: success`, buyer `0xaad1566216D2447B530E04945dfEefD04C84967B`; Solana Devnet
`am8frLb4uYjUiAa96gdyqzXvHgPpaWWYb5UVu8S7vCn44VYbBE7BYmKKLT8ZuTTZ1uK1xzrspH94VZPxUQ4C9sH` and
`3vRmDQNfXsr3VhsoUhMoXtkHunQ83FAFbrNMSrmxHGjkFWGdGsfUTjyqwe2LpjLdpAMjGnWj1hdb2X5rCMrquSQ8`,
buyer `9VyoFEDVasUjrfugJ6xd2fGwXjfAuHLBBxVGgZ8wnqqm`. The on-chain verification scripts S18 lost
to an ephemeral scratchpad were rewritten; they also re-confirmed S18's own two Solana
signatures on the same wallet, which is what makes the two sessions' evidence comparable. The
identifier the CLI now prints was checked against the chain and **matches the real transfer**.

**The four documented install rows were each run end to end**, in fresh environments built
from the page's own commands, each with only that chain's key exported: TypeScript Base,
TypeScript Solana, Python `[evm]`, Python `[svm]` — all four returned `200`. That is the
property O72 and O73 demanded and it is now a measured fact rather than a reading of the page.

**Regressions were run against `7376245` first.** `packages/tx402/test/ux-regressions-s18.test.ts`
fails 8 of 11 there and passes 11 of 11 after; the Python twin fails 5 of 6 there and passes 6
of 6 after. The assertions that pass in both states are deliberate do-not-regress guards — the
SPEC §11 stdout/stderr split, and the event stream keeping `settlementIdHash` hashed, which is
the half of ADR-019 that must **not** change.

**The documentation was published manually, on the user's explicit in-session
authorization, and this is a precondition for S20 rather than a convenience.** Measured
before the deploy: `docs.tx402.io` was still serving the **pre-S19** quickstart — the
both-chain step-6 snippet live, the scaffold line and the exit-9 row absent. §11.3 sends a
cold agent to read the documentation site, so starting S20 against that site would have made
it re-find O72, O73 and O76 — three findings that are already fixed — and the re-run would
have failed for reasons that are no longer true. That is the same trap S17 defused before
S18, and it recurs every time a remediation session changes the docs.

Rebuilt from the committed tree (`docs:check` current, 17 pages) and deployed with
`wrangler pages deploy docs/dist --project-name tx402-docs --branch main`, the same project
and command the workflow uses, from the user's own authenticated Cloudflare account —
deployment `ddbc4b4b`, source `b313d99`, Production on `main`. The production alias lagged
the deployment by roughly half a minute; both the deployment URL and `docs.tx402.io` were
polled until they agreed, so "published" here means verified over the public internet rather
than inferred from a successful upload. Confirmed afterwards: `docs:live` **8 / 8**, and ten
specific S19 markers present on the live quickstart and CLI guide, including the scaffold
line, both runner commands, the chain-scoped snippet titles, the exit-9 row and the ADR-019
reference. The `.wrangler/` scratch directory was removed and the tree left clean.

**This does not close O56.** The repository still cannot deploy itself: the credentials live
only on one developer's machine, the workflow still has no `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, and the
next push to `main` will fail exactly the same way. A manual deploy is a published site, not
a pipeline.

**CI is measured on `main`, not inherited.** `main` was fast-forwarded to `15284eb` and
pushed directly — no pull request, consistent with every previous session on this repository
— to `jaykomarraju/tx402-dev`, which is **not** `neogeeks/tx402`; the standing instruction
not to touch the public repository is unaffected. **CI run #50 on `15284eb` is 11 / 11
green**: Docs site, conformance parity, TypeScript on Node 20 and 22, Python 3.10 / 3.11 /
3.12 / 3.13, macOS and Windows cross-platform legs, and Release gates (fuzz, perf, supply
chain). For the first time this figure was read from the GitHub API rather than copied from a
commit message; the baseline was checked the same way and **run #49 on `7376245` was likewise
11 / 11**, which retrospectively substantiates the claim S18 and S19 were both handed.

**Docs run #7 fails as designed**, exactly as #6 did: checkout, install, SDK build,
generated-page currency, site build and deployable-root all succeed, and only
`A deploy from main requires the deploy credentials` fails, with all three deploy steps
skipped. That is O56's fail-closed condition and **not** a product regression. The site
itself is already current, from the manual deploy recorded above.

**§11.3 is still not discharged, and S19 cannot discharge it.** A remediation session is not a
re-run. Under §11.1 the pass must be re-run cold by an agent that has not read `PLAN.md`,
`SPEC.md`, `PRD.md`, `adr/` or any source file — exactly as S17's fixes did not discharge
S18's pass. Only a re-run returning **zero** findings advances the project to §11.4.

### S20 — fresh-eyes UX pass re-run (§11.3): cold start, findings only — **did not come back clean**

2026-08-05, 16:07–16:25Z. A second cold re-run, by an agent that had read none of `PLAN.md`,
`SPEC.md`, `PRD.md`, `adr/` or any source file, against the published site.

**Five findings, filed here as O77–O81. Two are HIGH.** None is a re-find: O72–O76 all stayed
fixed, and the pass confirmed it — all four language x chain combinations delivered settled
payments, the `settlement.transaction` O74 added was verified against the chain in two
scenarios, and the exit-9 reconciliation advice O76 added was judged "genuinely actionable".

**Two of the five were within S19's blast radius, and S19 missed them.** O77 is O72's root
cause — an optional chain dependency failing inside a `catch` that blames the environment
variable — in a second location, the CLI's own dev-key loader. S19 fixed the documentation
half and never ran the CLI in the missing-package configuration that O72 was about. O79 is
worse: S19 _observed_ that `tx402/signers` imports `viem/accounts` at module scope, reasoned
about it, judged it out of scope, and then neither fixed nor reported it. Out-of-scope was
defensible; silence was not. One O81 instance was introduced by S19 outright.

**Measured time to value: 2 min 07 s** on Base Sepolia from install command to on-chain
confirmation, of which roughly 1 min 52 s was investigating O77 — the documented path itself
executed in about 10 s, and Python went venv-to-settled-payment in 23 s. SPEC §16's
five-minute claim holds on the narrow path, and does not hold at all for a reader who follows
step 3's "Setting both is fine", whose clock is unbounded because the error sends them to fix
a key that was already correct.

**S20 was findings-only and wrote nothing to the repository.**

### S21 — S20 remediation: all five findings closed

2026-08-05, on branch `fix/s21-ux-remediation`. **All five of O77–O81 are resolved**, in both
languages wherever the item is language-bearing.

**O77 and O79 are one defect in one function**, `resolveSigners`, and were fixed together. A
module-resolution failure is now distinguished from a key-validation failure, and the affected
chain is **skipped with a named warning** rather than aborting the run. The install commands
come from the existing table that `tools/install-contract` already holds to the package's own
`peerDependencies`, so the advice cannot drift from what the package manager would actually
do. Skipping is safe in the direction that matters: not offering a signer can only ever remove
a payment option, never redirect one.

**O78 required a decision and is recorded as ADR-020.** `plan()` deliberately keeps raising —
that raise is the documented exit-5 cause the CLI's `--dry-run` depends on, and a route
ranking computed without an address would be a fabrication. The examples were calling the
wrong method: `inspect()` decodes and validates the challenge, touches no chain, and is
exactly the keyless capability they had been promising. `examples/README.md` needed no edit;
its claims became true rather than being softened.

**Verified against real money, in the configurations the findings named.** A Base Sepolia
payment with **both** keys exported and only the Base row installed — the O77 configuration —
settled as `0x4a8fd3fdaadd18499bdaadcaee678cac3dc099aac1ede98225823ee44511efe2`. A Solana
Devnet payment from a Python environment with **only** the `svm` extra and both keys exported
— the Python O77 configuration, which S20 found broke the quickstart's own Solana row —
settled as
`3S1ZTzjB9jKB9YGnHcGDMRtGy5w1gCnAXiqRA83KboizYkDs4G4HKMjcP6DMG8wAfq12ZArsTjmJsRsfzoVBiFCF`.
Both confirmed on-chain, 50000 atomic, buyers `0xaad1566216D2447B530E04945dfEefD04C84967B`
and `9VyoFEDVasUjrfugJ6xd2fGwXjfAuHLBBxVGgZ8wnqqm`. Both dry-run examples were run against a
live merchant with no key set at all: exit 0 in both languages.

**Regressions were run against `ee587df` first**, where the TypeScript suite fails 5 of 8 and
the Python twin 2 of 3. The assertions passing in both states are do-not-regress guards — that
a genuinely malformed key is still reported as one, in each language, because collapsing the

**Gates measured.** `pnpm check` green on Node v22.23.1 (569 passed, 11 skipped; size 17.27 /
25.00 and 32.00 / 32.00 KiB gz), Python gates green (576 passed, 92.64 % coverage).

**CI and the publish, both measured.** `main` was fast-forwarded to `32ca84a` and pushed

`A deploy from main requires the deploy credentials` fails, with the deploy steps skipped.
That is O56 fail-closed and not a regression.

**The documentation was rebuilt and published**, on the user's standing in-session
authorization, because S21 changed six pages and §11.3 sends a cold agent to read the site —

**Cloudflare Pages propagates per file and staggers.** A sweep run immediately after the

Recorded because a verification run immediately after a deploy can produce a false positive,

### S22 — fresh-eyes UX pass re-run (§11.3): cold start, findings only — **did not come back clean**

**Four findings, filed here as O82–O85. Three MEDIUM, one LOW.**

**No defect was found in either SDK's behaviour.** Every product guarantee the pass tried to

### S23 — S22 remediation: all four findings closed, and O79 reopened and finished

2026-08-05, on branch `fix/s23-ux-remediation`. **All four of O82–O85 are resolved**, in both
languages wherever the item is language-bearing.

**O79 was not resolved and its row said it was.** The finding named two defects — a false
documentation claim and a raw crash — and its own filing text says it "additionally requires a
documentation correction". S21 fixed the crash, left the claim standing, and closed the item;
the claim was still on the quickstart for S22 to find, which re-filed it as O82 and
additionally found the landing page repeating the pattern structurally. O79's row now records
the crash half only and points at O82. **That is the failure mode this project keeps
reproducing: a remediation session scoping itself to the part of a finding it found easiest to
verify, and then trusting its own scope.**

**Two of the four were introduced by earlier remediation.** S19 wrote O83's exit-9 prose while
fixing a different exit-9 item. S21 added O85's example headers **in the same commit** that
removed thirteen instances of that exact class from `docs/` — and its own regression could not
see them, for two independent reasons: it walked `docs/src/content/docs/` only, with `EXAMPLES`
bound as a constant and never swept, and it matched `session S12` and `open item O3` rather
than the bare `S21` and `(O78)` the examples actually carried.

**The guard is widened rather than duplicated.** `packages/tx402/test/reader-surfaces.ts` is
now the single definition of "text a reader reads" — the site, `examples/`, the repository
README, and each package's own README — and both the S20 and S23 suites sweep it. A fourth
narrowly-scoped test would have been the third instance of the same mistake. The bare-identifier
patterns catch exactly the two O85 instances and nothing else across every surface: zero false
positives, verified before the fix was applied.

**O83 is a prose defect against correct code, and was fixed as one.** `--scenario
unsuccessful-settlement` and `--scenario always-402` both exit `9` with `settlement: null`,
`context.paid: false` and `details.reason` of `settlement-unsuccessful` /
`max-paid-attempts-exhausted` — and an on-chain delta of **zero**, measured either side against
Base Sepolia USDC rather than taken from tool output: 17,798,000 atomic before and after both
runs. The control is `--scenario settled-but-refused`, which exits `9` with
`paid: true` and a committed settlement, transaction
`0x8612ff12517a32f3c39af26180e530b178eec0263d3888b8e05454b029ee9af3`, receipt status `0x1` in
block 45,091,804, moving exactly 50,000 atomic. So exit 9 covers both halves of a range whose
halves want **opposite actions**, and the docs described only one of them while sitting in the
quickstart's troubleshooting table. Neither the exit mapping nor the JSON was touched; the
prose now describes what the code already did, and `context.paid` — the field that carries the
distinction, mentioned on neither page before — is now the thing both pages send a reader to.
A behavioural regression pins the code so the sentence cannot later be made true in the wrong
direction.

**O84 required a decision and is recorded as ADR-021.** The two halves are not one defect.
`timeouts.initialRequestMs` is a **SPEC §4.3** field, and §4.3 is a language-neutral normative
table, so a Python client with no spelling for it was a conformance gap — closed in code, as
`initial_request_timeout_ms` on both Python clients, bounding the unpaid request only. The
budget-state split is required of TypeScript by SPEC §4.1 and of Python by **nothing** (§4.2's
export table names no budget accessor), so it is documented per language rather than mirrored
into an API no specification asks for. Narrowing the documentation for the first half would
have left a normative field unimplemented and merely stopped saying so; adding a Python
snapshot for the second would have put new mutable state on a money path for symmetry with a
language.

**Regressions were run against `03f368f` first**, where the TypeScript suite fails **13 of 15**
and the Python twin **5 of 6**. The three that pass in both states are labelled as such and are
not evidence: the behavioural pin on exit 9, the guard that the examples keep their
`inspect()`-vs-`plan()` explanation rather than being over-deleted, and the pin that Python's
budget surface stays deliberately singular.

**O82 reproduced in both languages before the fix and after.** A core-only install from the
packed tarball into an empty directory, key exported, `--dry-run` → exit `5`,
`TX402_SCHEME_UNSUPPORTED`; the Python wheel behaves identically with
`Run: pip install "tx402[evm]"`. The behaviour is correct and stays; step 1 no longer
contradicts step 4 of its own page, the landing page's install block now carries a chain row,
and `security/keys` now points the "find out what it costs without being able to pay" advice at
`client.inspect()`, which actually satisfies it, rather than at `--dry-run`, which needs both a
key and an adapter.

**Gates measured, all re-measured from scratch rather than inherited.** `pnpm check` exit 0 on
Node v22.23.1 — **584 passed, 11 skipped** (569/11 at `03f368f`, +15 from the new suite); size
gate **17.27 / 25.00 KiB gz** own code and **32.00 / 32.00 KiB gz** core import path, both
unchanged to the byte, because the only TypeScript source edit was a comment. Python gates exit
0 — ruff, ruff format over 49 files, mypy strict over 25 source files, **582 passed at 92.92 %
coverage** against a 90 % floor (576 at 92.64 % before).

**CI at `03f368f` verified independently before starting**, from the GitHub API rather than
inherited: **run #53 on `03f368f` is 11 / 11**, and **Docs run #8 fails as designed** — steps 1
through 9 pass, only `A deploy from main requires the deploy credentials` fails, and the three
deploy steps are skipped. That is O56 fail-closed, not a regression.

**§11.3 is still not discharged, and S23 cannot discharge it.** Three consecutive cold re-runs

the previous remediation had touched. The session after this one must be another cold
fresh-eyes re-run; only a zero-finding result advances the project to §11.4.

**CI and the publish, both measured after the fact.** `main` was fast-forwarded to `743719f` and

**The documentation was rebuilt and published**, on the user's in-session authorization, because

### S24 — fresh-eyes UX pass re-run (§11.3): cold start, findings only — **did not come back clean**

`PRD.md`, `adr/` or any source file, against the published site.

### S25 — S24 remediation: all six findings closed, and one S23 decision reversed

2026-08-05, on branch `fix/s25-ux-remediation`. **All six of O86–O91 are resolved.**

**O86 is the one that mattered.** The policy guide taught three ways to write a cap and marked
two of them valid; one had never been accepted by either language. The immediate failure is
loud, which held it at MEDIUM — but the repair a reader reaches for is silent and catastrophic:
told `100000` means atomic units, they add the symbol, and `"100000 USDC"` parses cleanly to a
cap **one million times** the one they meant. Spend caps are this product's headline safety
property and this is the page the landing page links as "the guardrails". The false line is now
marked `✗` with its real `reason`, and the near-miss has its own danger callout naming the exact
magnitude. Introduced in `ea9935c`, the original documentation-site commit — it survived four
cold passes.

**The guard class is the lesson, not the six items.** S23 widened the reader-facing sweep to
every surface a reader reads, and it still could not see O86 or O88, because it swept for
_internal citations_ — text that resolves to nothing outside the repository. A page claiming a
**capability the binary does not have** is invisible to any wording rule. So the new guards
execute or compare instead of pattern-matching: the policy guide's money examples are **run**
and their `✓`/`✗` markers checked against what the client does with each value; the lifecycle
guide's event enumeration is **compared** against the set the source emits and against Python's
exported `EVENT_NAMES`; and shell blocks a page tells you to run from the repository root have
their `require()` targets **resolved** from the repository root.

**That last guard caught itself being useless, which is worth recording.** Its first draft
passed against `75c1c98` — the commit whose defect it existed to catch. Vitest exports a
`NODE_PATH` into the pnpm store and everything the suite spawns inherits it, so under the test
runner `require('viem/accounts')` resolves from any directory on the machine. Both the
in-process resolve and the spawned command were therefore incapable of failing. They now clear
`NODE_PATH` explicitly, and the test asserts a **proven negative** — that `viem` really is
unresolvable from the root — so that the probe losing its teeth is itself a failure. A guard
that cannot fail is worse than no guard, because it is counted.

**O91 reverses a decision this project made deliberately three commits earlier**, and is
recorded as an amendment to ADR-021 rather than a quiet edit. The original reasoning was
cross-language diagnostic consistency (ADR-005): report the SPEC field name so the same mistake
reads identically in both languages. It loses on two counts. It was inconsistent _within_
Python — every other Python `configPath` uses the Python spelling, including this field's own
sibling `payment_retry_timeout_ms`, left untouched in the same change — and it named a path the
caller could neither have typed nor switch to, since `Tx402Client` accepts no `timeouts`
argument and the configuration page written in that same commit says so outright. ADR-005 is
not weakened: the error **code** and **reason** remain identical across languages, and those are
what a program branches on. `configPath` is a human-facing pointer into one language's own
configuration surface.

**Regressions were run against `75c1c98` first**, where the TypeScript suite fails **10 of 13**
and the Python twin **3 of 4**. The four that pass in both states are labelled and are not
evidence: the regex-sanity check, the reverse-direction event check, the cross-language event
agreement, and the sibling-field do-not-regress guard.

**Gates measured, re-measured from scratch rather than inherited.** `pnpm check` exit 0 on Node
v22.23.1 — **597 passed, 11 skipped** (584 / 11 at `75c1c98`, +13 from the new suite); size gate own-code **17.27 / 25.00 KiB gz** and core import path
**32.00 / 32.00 KiB gz**, both unchanged. Python gates exit 0 — ruff, ruff format over 50 files,
mypy strict over 25 source files, **586 passed at 92.92 % coverage** against a 90 % floor.

**CI at `75c1c98` verified independently before starting:** run **#55 is 11 / 11**, read from
the GitHub API.

**CI and the publish, both measured after the fact.** `main` was fast-forwarded to `920ac0d`
and pushed to `jaykomarraju/tx402-dev` — not `neogeeks/tx402`. **CI run #56 on `920ac0d` is
11 / 11 green**, read from the GitHub API. **Docs run #10 fails as designed**, step for step
identical to #8 and #9: only `A deploy from main requires the deploy credentials` fails, with
the three deploy steps skipped. That is O56 fail-closed and not a regression.

**The documentation was rebuilt and published**, on the user's authorization — deployment
`2c557b87`, source `920ac0d`. Verified over the public internet afterwards: `docs:live`
**8 / 8**, an internal-citation sweep across **all sixteen** live pages returns clean, and each
of the six corrections was checked against the live bytes rather than the built `dist/`.
**Propagation staggered harder than at S21 or S23** — four sweeps over roughly eighty seconds
before every page agreed, with `operations/publishing` the last to settle and one page briefly
serving new bytes for one assertion and old bytes for another. Polling until every claim agrees,
rather than until the first one does, is the form of this check that works.

**The payment path was smoke-tested after the edits**, on Node v20.19.5 against the packed
tarball: exit 0, `"events": 9`, settlement committed. Two settlements, not one — the first
invocation redirected stderr into the JSON file, so its document could not be parsed even
though it had exited 0 and paid. Both were recovered from Base Sepolia `Transfer` logs rather
than from tool output: `0xc5925c77809623047733fbbe75c5431b7299966b79c6a4580ad18ca1c5a01be5`
(block 45,094,535) and `0x64c0c3b866b768f3f09c9adef91fe65b9065a9777c2ba56af415c166d0075aed`
(block 45,094,540), 50,000 atomic each. Balance reconciles exactly: 17,298,000 → 17,198,000.
Solana Devnet untouched at **29,198,000**. Recorded including the double-spend of testnet funds,
because a settled payment that the operator could not read is exactly the situation the exit-code
documentation exists for.

**§11.3 is still not discharged, and S25 cannot discharge it.** Four consecutive cold re-runs
have now returned findings. The trend is real and worth stating plainly: S22 and S24 both found

2026-08-05. A fifth cold re-run, against the published site.

**Three findings, filed here as O92–O94. One moderate, two low.** A fourth, **O95**, was found
